洛杉矶县发现的狂犬病蝙蝠数量创历史新高 - ABC7 Los Angeles

Record number of rabid bats detected in Los Angeles County - ABC7 Los Angeles
新闻简报洛杉矶县今年已确认有 69 只患有狂犬病的蝙蝠,与去年的记录持平,促使公共卫生官员在世界 狂犬病 日发出警告。该警告于 2026 年 9 月 29 日发布,敦促居民采取预防措施,尤其是在蝙蝠旺季仍在继续的情况下。十多年来,狂犬病蝙蝠的检测数量一直在增加,仅 8 月份就有 24 只蝙蝠得到确诊,32% 的受检蝙蝠检测结果呈阳性。在 69 例病例中,68% 发生在圣克拉丽塔山谷,表明存在局部趋势。洛杉矶县公共卫生部强调,蝙蝠咬伤由于体积小而不易被注意到,而且一旦出现症状,狂犬病 几乎总是致命的。官员们建议不要触摸蝙蝠或其他野生动物,建议设置防蝙蝠的房屋,并敦促如果在室内或睡觉的人附近发现蝙蝠,请立即与医疗服务提供者或公共卫生部门联系。暴露后预防 可用于预防接触后的 狂犬病,并鼓励居民保持宠物的 疫苗接种 为最新状态。目前情况仍在积极调查和管理中。
查看新闻五要素
时间
洛杉矶县今年确认了 69 只患有狂犬病的蝙蝠,与去年的记录持平。事件日期没有明确说明,但警告是在每年 9 月 28 日的世界 狂犬病 日发布的。报告提到,仅 8 月份就有 24 只蝙蝠被确诊患有狂犬病,8 月份检测的蝙蝠中有 32% 呈阳性。发布日期为2026年9月29日。
地点与对象
洛杉矶县,包括圣克拉丽塔谷、圣费尔南多谷和洛杉矶市中心。受影响人群包括居民、儿童、宠物以及可能接触过狂犬病蝙蝠的个人。报告还提到,蝙蝠的遭遇发生在办公楼、公园、学校、企业和住宅后院。
事件
公共卫生官员在世界 狂犬病 日发出警告,敦促居民采取预防措施。该事件得到了洛杉矶县公共卫生部的证实。
规模、影响与风险
十多年来,狂犬病蝙蝠的检测量一直呈上升趋势。仅8月份,就有24只蝙蝠被确诊患有狂犬病,其中32%的受检蝙蝠检测结果呈阳性。今年发现的 69 只患有狂犬病的蝙蝠中,68% 是在圣克拉丽塔山谷发现的。暴露在室内的风险更高,大约三分之一的蝙蝠遭遇发生在室内。一旦出现症状,狂犬病 几乎总是致命的。
应对、状态与不确定性
公共卫生官员已发出警告和建议,包括敦促居民报告在室内或室外发现的任何蝙蝠,如果它看起来生病、白天活跃、无法飞行或死亡。他们还建议不要接触蝙蝠或其他野生动物,并推荐防蝙蝠之家。县卫生官员强调了 暴露后预防 和保持宠物 狂犬病 疫苗接种 最新的重要性。这种情况正在持续发生并正在积极管理中。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Record number of rabid bats detected in Los Angeles County - ABC7 Los Angeles

News Brief
Los Angeles County has confirmed 69 rabid bats this year, matching last year's record and prompting public health officials to issue warnings on World Rabies Day. The warning, issued on September 29, 2026, urges residents to take precautions, especially as peak bat season continues. Rabid bat detections have been rising for over a decade, with 24 confirmed in August alone and 32% of tested bats testing positive. Of the 69 cases, 68% were found in the Santa Clarita Valley, indicating a localized trend. The Los Angeles County Department of Public Health emphasized that bat bites can be unnoticed due to their small size and that rabies is almost always fatal once symptoms appear. Officials advised against touching bats or other wild animals, recommended bat-proofing homes, and urged immediate contact with medical providers or Public Health if a bat is found indoors or near sleeping individuals. Post-exposure prophylaxis is available to prevent rabies following exposure, and residents are encouraged to keep pets' vaccinations up to date. The situation remains under active investigation and management.
Original source text (English)
24/7 LiveLos AngelesOrange CountyInland EmpireVentura CountyCalifornia 100° HEALTH & FITNESS Record number of rabid bats detected in Los Angeles County Los Angeles County has confirmed 69 rabid bats this year, matching last year's record and prompting public health officials to urge residents to take precautions as peak bat season continues. Tuesday, September 29, 2026 6:01AM Share Follow ABC7 on Google LOS ANGELES (CNS) -- Los Angeles County has confirmed 69 rabid bats this year, matching last year's record and prompting public health officials Monday to urge residents to take precautions as peak bat season continues. The Los Angeles County Department of Public Health issued the warning on World Rabies Day, which is observed annually Sept. 28. Rabid bat detections have been trending upward in the county for more than a decade, according to the department. Twenty-four rabid bats were confirmed in August alone, and 32% of bats submitted by residents for rabies testing during the month tested positive. Of the 69 rabid bats detected this year, 68% were found in the Santa Clarita Valley, continuing a trend seen during the past decade. Most were Canyon Bats, suggesting rabies may be circulating more intensely in that local bat population, according to Public Health. "Bats are an important part of our environment, but they can be infected with and spread rabies, a disease that is almost always fatal once symptoms appear," county Health Officer Dr. Muntu Davis said in a statement. "If you find a bat indoors or think you may have had contact with one, contact your medical provider or Public Health right away. And remember, never touch a bat or any wild animals. Immediately report any bat found indoors, or outdoors if it appears sick, active during the day, unable to fly, or dead, to your local animal control." Public Health officials said rabid bats have been found throughout Los Angeles County, including the San Fernando Valley and downtown Los Angeles, and in locations including office buildings, parks, schools, businesses and residential backyards. About one-third of bat encounters occur indoors, where the risk of exposure is higher. Exposure to a rabid bat or a bat that might have rabies can occur when saliva or tissue from the animal enters the body through a bite or scratch, or comes into contact with a wound, mouth, nose or eyes. Because bat bites can be very small and may feel like a pinprick, they can go unnoticed, according to Public Health. Health officials said anyone who wakes up with a bat in the room or finds one near a sleeping person, child or pet should consider it a possible exposure and contact animal control or Public Health. Officials advised residents not to release such bats so they can be safely collected and tested. Rabies can be prevented following an exposure with post-exposure prophylaxis, a series of timely vaccines, according to Public Health. Residents were also urged to keep their pets' rabies vaccinations current, avoid touching bats and other wild animals and report bats that are active during the day, on the ground or unable to fly. Officials also recommended that residents bat-proof their homes by sealing openings, installing heavy-duty wire mesh over vents, ensuring exterior doors close tightly and using window screens and chimney caps.
